## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for optional questions where the answer would materially improve the quality of the work.

## developer

<multi_agent_role>You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

# AGENTS.md

This file is the Codex-facing adapter for this repository. It summarizes the Claude-oriented project guidance from `CLAUDE.md` in a smaller format that is fast for Codex to scan. `CLAUDE.md` remains the fuller source of project knowledge.

## Fast Locator

- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`
- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`
- Live vault knowledge: `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/wiki/` (hubs, voice, business), `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/students/`, `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/corpus/`
- Pre-2026-07-16 LSATU product knowledge (archived, read-only): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`

If an older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat it as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.

## Project Map

- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.
- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.
- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.
- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.
- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.
- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.
- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.
- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.

## Stack And Commands

- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.
- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.
- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.
- Build: `npm run build`
- Lint: `npm run lint`
- Test: `npm run test`
- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`
- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.
- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).

## Working Rules

- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.
- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.
- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.
- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.
- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.
- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.
- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.
- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.

## Product Constraints

- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.
- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.
- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.
- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.
- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.

## Smart Drill Note

On `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.

## Verification (must not skip)

- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not "done" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.
- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed "fix" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.

## Workflow Expectations

- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.
- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.
- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.
- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.
- **After every successful push, publish through Lovable's manual Publish control.** Do not prompt the Lovable agent, use a credit-spending action, or change anything else in Lovable. Verify that the manual publish completed before reporting the change as live.
- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.

## Session Protocol (second brain)

Product context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.

The vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student's record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.

- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.
- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong "Done" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.

`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.

## Codex Adapter Layout

- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.
- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.
- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.

This checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.

## Known Gaps

- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.
- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.
- `README.md` is still minimal.

## Agent skills

### Issue tracker

GitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.

### Triage labels

The five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.

### Domain docs

Multi-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.

Student copy follows `wiki/teaching-voice.md`; no em dashes.\\r\\n- Hard rules: completed student work is never lost or modified (optional fields, fallbacks, no destructive migration). One branch per session, PR to main, never commit to main. Lovable Cloud backend: Edge Functions deploy through Lovable, not the Supabase CLI. Frontend reaches lsatprep.study only via Lovable deploy. A teacher is never the student: rows addressed to the coach account are not assigned work.\\r\\n- Separate, already done: PR #132 fixes the sideways scrollbar (glow overflow).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Goal\\r\\nA sleek, high-end Home that (1) gets a student into practice in one click, (2) shows the one performance insight that matters most for THIS student\'s recent work, and (3) puts every message and notification in one place.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Approach\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Layout (top to bottom, desktop two-column above ~1100px, single column below)\\r\\n1. **Command bar**: Question finder (existing `QuestionFinder`, PrepTest / Section / Question dropdowns plus free text) + inbox bell + greeting line.\\r\\n2. **Three one-click actions**: Continue assigned work (next due item; hidden when none), Smart Drill, Start a timed section.\\r\\n3. **Performance area**: one main insight card + two or three small insight cards. Chosen automatically; Joshua can pin.\\r\\n4. **Inbox panel** (right column on desktop): one list, Joshua\'s messages pinned on top.\\r\\n5. **Next session** card (dark, anchors the right column).\\r\\n6. **Next seven days**: the CURRENT strip, byte-for-byte unchanged in markup and behavior (Joshua: \\"I don\'t want you to change anything\\").\\r\\n- Look: light and warm, with dark cards for emphasis (Next session, main insight). Motion: count-up numbers, card settle-in, gentle pulse; all disabled under `prefers-reduced-motion`.\\r\\n- Empty states: one line plus one action per card.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Insight engine (fixed rules, no AI text)\\r\\n- Pure function `pickInsights(signals, pin?) -> { main, minor[] }` in a new `src/components/classroom/home/insights.ts`, unit-tested with fixtures.\\r\\n- Candidate lenses, each computed from existing data:\\r\\n  - Question types: `attempt_accuracy_rollup(p_class_id)` (qtype, correct, total), last 30 days, min 8 questions per type.\\r\\n  - Sections: `section_history` (initial_score/total, br_score/br_total, br_delta, time_taken_ms, pt, section, completed_at).\\r\\n  - Assigned work results: existing assigned-work feed (`useAssignedTodoFeed`) completion + scores.\\r\\n  - Bootcamp progress: bootcamp module completion.\\r\\n  - Repeat mistakes: `wrong_answer_journal` grouped by qtype / trap.\\r\\n  - Pace: `attempts.time_ms` vs type median where accuracy is high.\\r\\n  - Confidence gaps: `attempts.confidence` high + `correct` false.\\r\\n- Rules: score each lens by recency and volume of the student\'s activity in its source over the last 14 days; highest = main, next 2-3 = minor. A lens with insufficient data is ineligible (never show a thin number).\\r\\n- Each insight carries a one-click action (e.g. weak type -> Smart Drill filtered to that type).\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Pinning (teacher control)\\r\\n- New table `home_insight_pins` (`class_id` PK, `lens` text, `params` jsonb, `pinned_by`, `updated_at`), RLS: admin write, student read own row. Additive migration only.\\r\\n- Teacher sets a pin from the student\'s Hub page. Pinned lens becomes main; engine fills the minors.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 4. Unified inbox\\r\\n- One hook `useInbox()` merging (a) coach messages (existing Messages/Inbox source) and (b) `notifications` (types today: assignment, message, reminder, streak, signup) into one time-sorted list; Joshua\'s messages pinned first.\\r\\n- Kinds shown: Joshua\'s messages, new assignments, graded work, session reminders, booking changes, new notes and recaps. Kinds that no writer emits yet (graded work, booking changes, notes/recaps if missing) need writers added where the event happens.\\r\\n- Surfaces: Home panel + a bell in the Classroom frame header opening the same list (popover). Mark-read shared.\\r\\n- `streak` and `signup` types are hidden from students (streak removed from Home by decision; signup is teacher-facing).\\r\\n\\r\\n### 5. Build order (each its own PR)\\r\\n1. Layout + restyle using existing data only (command bar, actions, inbox panel reading messages only, next session, week strip untouched).\\r\\n2. Insight engine with question-type and section lenses.\\r\\n3. Unified inbox + bell + missing notification writers.\\r\\n4. Remaining lenses + pinning (table, Hub control).\\r\\n5. Teacher view of Home adopts the design.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Key decisions & tradeoffs\\r\\n- Rules-based insight selection instead of AI text: predictable and never states something false; less \\"magic\\".\\r\\n- Additive table for pins instead of a column on `profiles`: keeps profiles lean, clean RLS; one more table.\\r\\n- Inbox merges at read time in the client instead of copying messages into `notifications`: no data duplication; two queries.\\r\\n- Week strip is frozen: design must fit around it.\\r\\n- Phased PRs over one big change: each ships alone and is reversible.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Risks / open questions\\r\\n- Do all practice modes (Smart Drill, timed sections, guided drills, bootcamps, RC) actually write `attempts` rows with `qtype`? If not, lenses undercount.\\r\\n- `attempt_accuracy_rollup` has no date filter today; a 30-day window may need a new RPC arg (additive).\\r\\n- Performance: Home already runs several queries; adding rollup + section_history + inbox merge may slow first paint. Skeletons and parallel fetch needed.\\r\\n- Notification writers for graded work and booking changes may live in Edge Functions (Lovable deploy path, confirm with Joshua first).\\r\\n- Mobile layout of two columns and the bell popover.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Proposed utility idea for Codex to sharpen (Joshua asked for ideas here)\\r\\nJoshua wants the dashboard to be more of a tool: \\"if you want a question, go specific question... maybe drop-down bar.\\" Draft idea: turn the command bar into a **Practice launcher** with three modes in one control:\\r\\n- **Open a question**: PrepTest / Section / Question dropdowns (exists today) plus recent questions.\\r\\n- **Drill a type**: question-type dropdown + count -> Smart Drill set.\\r\\n- **Timed section**: PrepTest + Section dropdown -> start the section.\\r\\nKeyboard shortcut (Ctrl+K) opens it from any Classroom page.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Out of scope\\r\\n- Public landing page (auth-landing): nothing borrowed from it.\\r\\n- Goal score, streaks, latest PT score, recent drills list, month calendar.\\r\\n- AI-written insight text.\\r\\n- Changes to how Smart Drill or assigned work run (already one question at a time).\\r\\n# Classroom Home visual upgrade: Brainstorm / Discovery Notes\\r\\nDate: 2026-09-23 � Goal: decide how to take the LSAT U Classroom Home (lsatprep.study/classroom) to another level visually\\r\\nStatus: in progress\\r\\nContext sources: live page inspected in the in-app browser (signed in as Joshua, teacher view); `projects/lsatu/src/components/classroom/home/` (ClassroomHomeView.tsx, home.css, homeModel.ts, useClassroomHome.ts); public landing `src/components/auth-landing/academy.tsx` for contrast\\r\\n\\r\\n## Inspection findings (verified 2026-09-23)\\r\\n- Look: warm cream background, orange accent, serif greeting (\\"Good morning, Joshua Fisseha\\"), mono uppercase eyebrows, soft drifting glows and a shine sweep.\\r\\n- Stack, top to bottom: search box, date eyebrow, greeting + status line, latest message preview (\\"FROM YAHUNATHAN\\", 2 NEW), \\"Start a section\\" RRC card, \\"Next session / Nothing booked\\" card, \\"Next seven days\\" strip.\\r\\n- Page ends right after the 7-day strip. Everything sits in one column at roughly 700px, so on a wide screen the right side is empty.\\r\\n- Bug: the decorative glows (`.classroom-home-glow-a`, `-glow-b`, `.classroom-home-shine`) extend past the viewport. scrollWidth 998 vs 907 viewport, so there is a horizontal scrollbar.\\r\\n- The public landing page is a different, dark \\"academy\\" look (marble bust, gold, point cloud). The Classroom is light and warm. The two do not share a visual language.\\r\\n- Not yet seen: the student view of Home (only the teacher view was inspected).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Summary / key decisions\\r\\n- 2026-09-23 round 7: Joshua can pin a view per student. Inbox as Home panel + bell everywhere, all six kinds, his messages pinned. Top actions: continue assigned work, Smart Drill, timed section; Question finder on top. Next seven days strip kept exactly as it is today.\\r\\n- 2026-09-23 round 6: the app picks the view automatically by fixed rules (no AI text); one main insight plus two or three small ones. No new one-question security rule; Home just gives quick access to Smart Drill and assigned work.\\r\\n- 2026-09-23 round 5: the performance area adapts to each student\'s work and assignments (types, sections, bootcamps, RC, or other lenses), not a fixed chart.\\r\\n- 2026-09-23 round 3: Home centers on section results, no goal score, one-question-at-a-time Smart Drill and assignments, fast access to questions and sections, one centralized inbox for messages and notifications. Sleek and high-end.\\r\\n- Light warm page, two dark anchor cards. Stats: latest PT, questions this week, sessions left, days to test, goal dial. Weakest types by name. Student view first. Restrained motion, reduced-motion safe. Scrollbar fixed separately. (2026-09-23)\\r\\n- Nothing is borrowed from the public landing page (auth-landing). The Classroom Home keeps its own identity. (Joshua, 2026-09-23)\\r\\n\\r\\n## Q&A log\\r\\n### Round 0 (unprompted)\\r\\n#### Q0 - Landing page influence\\r\\n- Captured: \\"I don\'t want to take anything from the authentic page.\\" (dictation for auth page). No visual borrowing from the dark academy landing.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Round 1 (asked, not answered directly)\\r\\nQ1 to Q6 were asked (student vs teacher first, feeling, desktop right column, visual vs new content, motion, scrollbar fix). Joshua replied with six reference dashboards instead. Read from that reply, tentatively: yes to more content and a multi-card layout (Q3, Q4). Q1, Q2, Q5, Q6 still open.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Round 2 - Reference board (Joshua, 2026-09-23)\\r\\nJoshua supplied six dashboards: \\"take inspiration from all of them and see what features we can incorporate\\", build components from each, then \\"a master version\\", and possibly redesign Home around these features. Use Claude Design.\\r\\n\\r\\nComponents pulled from each (Claude\'s reading, tentative until confirmed):\\r\\n1. **Notton (dark, mountain hero):** full-width atmospheric photo band under a top stat bar; row of four dark chart tiles (bars, donut, line, ranked list). Borrow: one image-led hero band, a row of equal-height data tiles.\\r\\n2. **Homies Lab (warm cream/yellow):** \\"Good Morning\\" greeting with the date under it, a stat row of 3-4 big numbers with small labels, a gauge dial on the right, a schedule card with tabs (Meetings/Tasks/Events), a line chart with an arrow button, small leave-balance tiles. Closest to the current palette. Borrow: big-number stat row in the hero, gauge, tabbed schedule card, mini metric tiles.\\r\\n3. **Coders (mono, black and white):** pill-shaped top widgets (time, weather, search), Gantt-style timeline, a dark \\"Team\\" block with progress bars. Borrow: black accent cards among white ones, pill widgets, a timeline row.\\r\\n4. **WeatherWise (dark sky photo):** huge light-weight number, a smooth curve across the week with a glowing dot on today, glass side panel. Borrow: the week strip as a curve with a glowing \\"today\\" point, big thin numerals.\\r\\n5. **Mountain chart dashboard (dark with white rail):** a light left rail of stacked numbers against a dark main area; charts that turn into landscape illustration. Borrow: charts with illustrative depth (mostly decorative).\\r\\n6. **Be.run (cream, black, yellow):** \\"Hi, Amanda!\\" greeting, overlapping blurred bubble chart, a dark month calendar with done/scheduled dots, a goal gauge, a progress bar with a marker, habit rows with segmented progress bars. Borrow: dark calendar card with done/scheduled states, a goal gauge, segmented progress per question type or bootcamp.\\r\\n\\r\\nData reality check: Home\'s model (homeModel.ts) currently has work rows, a hero task with progress, messages, next session, a booking link, and the seven-day week. No score trend, streak or per-type accuracy is wired to Home yet (the Analytics page holds that). Any stat tiles in the master design need a data source decision.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Master design v1 (Claude, 2026-09-23, tentative until Joshua reviews)\\r\\nCanvas: https://claude.ai/artifact/6zF3FgewiMA7pCZSk766Xt (two boards: Master Home, and What each reference gives us).\\r\\nMaster Home, top to bottom: pill top bar (search, days-to-test, streak) / hero card with greeting, four big stats and a goal gauge / three cards: Up next (progress bar with marker), Practice test trend (line + glowing point + goal line), dark Training days calendar / Where the points are (segmented bars per question type) beside From Joshua and a black Next session card / Next seven days drawn as a curve with a glowing today point.\\r\\nBuilt on the current tokens (cream #f8f1e7, orange #ff8906, ink #332f2c, Fraunces / Outfit / JetBrains Mono). All numbers are bracketed samples.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Round 2 answers (Joshua, 2026-09-23): \\"yes to all, go with your recommendations\\"\\r\\n- Q1 Direction: light and warm, with two dark cards (calendar, next session). No all-dark page.\\r\\n- Q2 Numbers on Home: latest PT score, questions this week, sessions left, days to test, goal dial. Cut streak and LR accuracy.\\r\\n- Q3 Weakest question types shown by name, leading into Smart Drill: yes.\\r\\n- Q4 Student Home first, teacher view follows: yes.\\r\\n- Q5 Motion: count-up numbers, cards settle in, gentle pulse on glowing dots; all off under prefers-reduced-motion.\\r\\n- Q6 Sideways scrollbar: fix now as a separate change on its own branch.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Data feasibility (sub-agent research, 2026-09-23, verified by file refs only)\\r\\n- Questions this week: EXISTS, daily_stats.questions_answered; sum last 7 days. Low effort.\\r\\n- Sessions left: EXISTS, useStudentBooking().pass.credits_remaining, already called on Home. Trivial.\\r\\n- Days to test: EXISTS, profiles.test_date (nullable) + existing daysUntil() helper. Low.\\r\\n- Weakest question types: EXISTS, RPC attempt_accuracy_rollup (used by Analytics). Low; needs a minimum-question floor.\\r\\n- Calendar study days: EXISTS via daily_stats rows with questions > 0; session dates from useStudentBooking bookings. Low.\\r\\n- Latest PT + trend: PARTIAL. practice_test_runs table exists but nothing writes it; in-app runs live in localStorage; imported scores in student_external_metrics (pt_score). Medium.\\r\\n- Goal and starting score: PARTIAL. Only bands in profiles.onboarding (target_score_band, last_score_band), can be \\"not sure\\".\\r\\n\\r\\n### Round 3 answers (Joshua, 2026-09-23) - supersedes parts of Round 2 Q2\\r\\n- \\"Focus on the section results instead of the latest PT score or recent drills.\\" Section results replace the PT card and trend. Data: section_history (initial_score/total, br_score/br_total, br_delta, time_taken_ms, by_qtype_json, pt, section, completed_at). Supersedes Round 2 Q2\'s \\"latest PT score\\".\\r\\n- \\"Don\'t include the goal score on the page.\\" Goal dial removed. Supersedes Round 2 Q2\'s goal dial. (Round 3 Q1 and Q2 about PT saving and exact goal score are now moot.)\\r\\n- Smart Drill: \\"an option to access the smart drill where they can click and open one question at a time for security\\", and the same for homework and assignments he assigns. One question opens at a time, never a whole set on screen.\\r\\n- \\"A sleek, high-end design that allows quick access to questions or sections for practice.\\"\\r\\n- \\"The messaging and notifications should be centralized in one place.\\" One inbox on Home for coach messages and system notifications.\\r\\n- Round 3 Q3 (empty states) not answered; Claude applies the recommendation (one line + one action) as tentative.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Facts checked before Round 4 (Claude, 2026-09-23)\\r\\n- A `notifications` table already exists (title, body, type, link, read) with `useNotifications` hook; written by classroomNotify, sessionBundleNotify, markNoteRead. Coach messages live separately (Inbox / Messages). Centralizing = one surface that reads both.\\r\\n- Joshua asked (via /grill-me) to keep refining before anything is built. The master design canvas update for round 3 is paused.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Round 4 (asked Q1-Q8; Joshua answered Q1 and Q2 together, 2026-09-23)\\r\\n#### Q1/Q2 - What the results area shows, and from which work\\r\\n- Recommended: last 3-5 full sections as cards (Q1a); full sections only (Q2).\\r\\n- Captured (supersedes Round 3 \\"focus on the section results\\"): \\"It doesn\'t have to be practice tests or sections. It can be more of a focus on something specific, like the performance on general question types.\\" It must include Smart Drill, sections, guided drills, \\"whatever they work with... really to be specific to them.\\" \\"It doesn\'t have to be limited to one type of information.\\"\\r\\n- Decision: the results area is a personal performance view by question type, fed by all of a student\'s work, not one source.\\r\\n- Fact (Claude checked): the `attempts` table records each answered question with `qtype`, `mode`, `correct`, `time_ms`, `confidence`; `attempt_accuracy_rollup` already sums it by type. Which modes write to `attempts` (Smart Drill, sections, guided drills, bootcamps) still to be confirmed per mode.\\r\\n#### Q3-Q8 - not yet answered (one-at-a-time rule and its reason, homework rule, inbox location, inbox contents, top 3 actions, what leaves Home)\\r\\n\\r\\n### Round 5 (asked Q1-Q9; Joshua answered the performance-view branch, 2026-09-23)\\r\\n#### Q1-Q3 - What the performance view shows\\r\\n- Recommended: accuracy + trend per question type, last 30 days, click opens Smart Drill on that type.\\r\\n- Captured (refines Round 4): \\"it can be different variations depending on what\'s useful for the student based on where they\'ve been assigned.\\" \\"It should depend on what they\'ve been doing and what they\'ve done.\\" \\"Whether it\'s based on their work history or something creative, it doesn\'t have to strictly be question types. These can be sections, question types, or any other format.\\"\\r\\n- Decision: the performance area is adaptive. It picks the lens that fits each student\'s recent work and assignments (question types, sections, bootcamp, RC passages, or other), rather than one fixed chart.\\r\\n- Open: how the lens is chosen, how many insights show, whether Joshua can steer it.\\r\\n#### Q4-Q9 - still unanswered (one-question rule reason, homework rule, inbox location and contents, top 3 actions, what leaves Home)\\r\\n\\r\\n### Round 6 answers (Joshua, 2026-09-23)\\r\\n#### Q1 - Who picks the view\\r\\n- Recommended: app chooses, Joshua can override.\\r\\n- Captured: \\"The app should choose.\\" Automatic. Override by Joshua was not confirmed.\\r\\n- Flags: whether Joshua wants a per-student override -> Joshua\\r\\n#### Q2 - How many views\\r\\n- Captured: recommendation accepted. One main insight, two or three smaller ones under it.\\r\\n#### Q3 - How the view is chosen\\r\\n- Captured: fixed rules. No AI-written line.\\r\\n#### Q4 - \\"One question at a time\\"\\r\\n- Captured: Smart Drill already is one question at a time. \\"I\'m not sure how smart drilling has to do with anything right now.\\" There is no new security rule to design.\\r\\n- Correction: Claude misread Round 3 (\\"for security\\") as a request for a new lockdown rule. Supersedes Round 3\'s Smart Drill line and Round 4 Q3/Q4 and Round 5 Q4/Q5; those questions are closed. What remains from Round 3 is quick access to Smart Drill and to assigned work from Home.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Round 7 answers (Joshua, 2026-09-23)\\r\\n#### Q1 - Override\\r\\n- Captured: automatic by default, \\"I want to have the option to pin stuff if I want to.\\" Joshua can pin a view for a student. Resolves the Round 6 override flag.\\r\\n#### Q2 - Inbox location\\r\\n- Captured: both. A panel on Home and a bell on every Classroom page, one shared list.\\r\\n#### Q3 - Inbox contents\\r\\n- Captured: recommendation. All six (Joshua\'s messages, new assignments, graded work, session reminders, booking changes, new notes and recaps), Joshua\'s messages pinned on top.\\r\\n#### Q4 - Top 3 actions\\r\\n- Captured: recommendation. Continue assigned work, Smart Drill, start a timed section. Open-a-specific-question stays in the top search bar (Question finder).\\r\\n#### Q5 - Next seven days\\r\\n- Captured: \\"The next seven days thing, I want to keep this there. That should not be removed in the design.\\" \\"I don\'t want you to change anything.\\" The current week strip stays exactly as it is today. Supersedes the curve version in master design v1.\\r\\n- Tentative (Claude): the month calendar from design v1 is dropped, since Joshua kept the current strip instead. Confirm.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Open flags (pending input)\\r\\n- Confirm every practice mode (Smart Drill, timed sections, guided drills, bootcamps, RC) writes to `attempts` -> Claude\\r\\n- Which stats appear on Home and where the numbers come from (Analytics data vs new queries) -> Joshua decides, Claude checks feasibility\\r\\n- Student-view screenshot of Home -> Claude (needs a student test account or impersonation route)\\r\\n"}'}]

Drives the \\"N NEW\\" badge; derived, never stored twice. */\\r\\n  isNew: boolean;\\r\\n  dueToday: boolean;\\r\\n  overdue: boolean;\\r\\n  /** Work the student can close out by hand; reading matter cannot. */\\r\\n  canComplete?: boolean;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * A timed section\'s instructions, carried on the row so Home can raise them\\r\\n   * over the stage rather than sending the student to a page to read them.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  briefing?: SectionBriefing;\\r\\n\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface HomeProgress {\\r\\n  /** Present only when both halves are provable. */\\r\\n  completed: number | null;\\r\\n  total: number;\\r\\n  /** \\"prompts\\", \\"questions\\" - plural noun for the count. */\\r\\n  unit: string;\\r\\n  estimateMinutes: number | null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface HomeHero {\\r\\n  row: HomeRow;\\r\\n  eyebrow: string;\\r\\n  /** \\"ACTIVITY\\", \\"GUIDED DRILL\\" - the mono tag beside the meta line. */\\r\\n  kindLabel: string;\\r\\n  /** The row\'s meta with the kind dropped: the tag beside it already says it. */\\r\\n  meta: string;\\r\\n  prose: string | null;\\r\\n  primaryLabel: string;\\r\\n  progress: HomeProgress | null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface HomeMessage {\\r\\n  id: string;\\r\\n  from: string;\\r\\n  initial: string;\\r\\n  timeLabel: string;\\r\\n  body: string;\\r\\n  unread: boolean;\\r\\n  route: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface HomeSession {\\r\\n  /** ISO start. Null renders the honest \\"Nothing booked\\" variant. */\\r\\n  startsAt: string;\\r\\n  durationMin: number | null;\\r\\n  label: string;\\r\\n  meta: string | null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** The plan-aware booking link shown next to the session card. */\\r\\nexport interface HomeBookingCta {\\r\\n  label: string;\\r\\n  href: string;\\r\\n  external: boolean;\\r\\n  detail: string | null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface HomeWeekDay {\\r\\n  iso: string;\\r\\n  abbr: string;\\r\\n  dayNum: number;\\r\\n  isToday: boolean;\\r\\n  isWeekend: boolean;\\r\\n  /** The single line under the date, or null for an empty day. */\\r\\n  label: string | null;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Where pressing this day goes, or null when the day has nothing to open.\\r\\n   *\\r\\n   * The route belongs to the FIRST thing on the day, which is the same thing\\r\\n   * `label` names. A cell reading \\"Read, Recall, Colour\\" that opened the\\r\\n   * schedule instead would be a link that lies about its own text.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  route: string | null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * What this particular person can actually reach.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Home is a shortcut into surfaces the rail already gates, so it has to gate\\r\\n * itself the same way: a student without chat access must not be shown a\\r\\n * message card that dead-ends on a locked route, and one without schedule\\r\\n * access gets a week band that reads but does not click. ONES[n] : String(n);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function greetingFor(now: Date = new Date()): string {\\r\\n  const hour = now.getHours();\\r\\n  if (hour < 12) return \\"Good morning\\";\\r\\n  if (hour < 18) return \\"Good afternoon\\";\\r\\n  return \\"Good evening\\";\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** \\"MONDAY � AUGUST 3 � 2026\\" - three beats, with the date kept whole. */\\r\\nexport function datelineFor(now: Date = new Date()): string {\\r\\n  const part = (options: Intl.DateTimeFormatOptions) =>\\r\\n    new Intl.DateTimeFormat(\\"en-CA\\", options).format(now);\\r\\n  return [\\r\\n    part({ weekday: \\"long\\" }),\\r\\n    part({ month: \\"long\\", day: \\"numeric\\" }),\\r\\n    part({ year: \\"numeric\\" }),\\r\\n  ].join(\\" � \\");\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The LOCAL calendar day a timestamp falls on.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * `iso.slice(0, 10)` is right for a bare `YYYY-MM-DD` and wrong for anything\\r\\n * carrying a time, because the stored timestamp is UTC. Every evening deadline in a western timezone hit this.\\r\\n */\\r\\nfunction calendarDay(iso: string): string {\\r\\n  if (/^\\\\d{4}-\\\\d{2}-\\\\d{2}$/.test(iso.trim())) return iso.trim();\\r\\n  const parsed = new Date(iso);\\r\\n  return Number.isNaN(parsed.getTime()) ? iso.slice(0, 10) : localDayISO(parsed);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction dayDiff(iso: string, today = localDayISO()): number | null {\\r\\n  const a = parseLocalDay(calendarDay(iso));\\r\\n  const b = parseLocalDay(today);\\r\\n  if (!a || !b) return null;\\r\\n  return Math.round((a.getTime() - b.getTime()) / 86_400_000);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** \\"due today\\" / \\"due tomorrow\\" / \\"overdue\\" / \\"due Friday\\" / \\"due 12 Sep\\". */\\r\\nexport function dueLabel(dueAt: string | null, now: Date = new Date()): string | null {\\r\\n  if (!dueAt) return null;\\r\\n  const diff = dayDiff(dueAt, localDayISO(now));\\r\\n  if (diff === null) return null;\\r\\n  if (diff < 0) return diff === -1 ? \\"one day overdue\\" : `${spell(-diff)} days overdue`;\\r\\n  if (diff === 0) return \\"due today\\";\\r\\n  if (diff === 1) return \\"due tomorrow\\";\\r\\n  if (diff <= 6) {\\r\\n    const date = parseLocalDay(calendarDay(dueAt));\\r\\n    return date\\r\\n      ? `due ${new Intl.DateTimeFormat(\\"en-CA\\", { weekday: \\"long\\" }).format(date)}`\\r\\n      : null;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  const date = {\\r\\n  const diff = dayDiff(createdAt, localDayISO(now));\\r\\n  if (diff === null) return null;\\r\\n  if (diff === 0) return \\"assigned today\\";\\r\\n  if (diff === -1) return \\"assigned yesterday\\";\\r\\n  if (diff < 0 && diff >= -6) return `assigned ${spell(-diff)} days ago`;\\r\\n  return `assigned ${new Intl.DateTimeFormat(\\"en-CA\\", {\\r\\n    day: \\"numeric\\",\\r\\n \\"activity\\",\\r\\n  bootcamp: \\"drill\\",\\r\\n  plan: \\"activity\\",\\r\\n  recap: \\"recap\\",\\r\\n  note: \\"recap\\",\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\nconst PRIMARY_LABEL: Record<HomeKind, { start: Falls back to the whole line when the kind is not the lead.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function stripKind(meta: string, kind: HomeKind): string {\\r\\n  const parts = meta.split(\\" � \\");\\r\\n  if (parts.length > 1 && parts[0] === KIND_LABEL[kind]) return parts.slice(1).join(\\" � \\");\\r\\n  return meta;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The one line under the greeting. Built from counts we can prove, and honest\\r\\n * when there is nothing: it never invents a streak or a projection.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function subtitleFor(dueToday: number, openWork: number, unread: number): string {\\r\\n  /** Every clause here opens a sentence, so the spelled number is capitalised. */\\r\\n  const Count = (n: number) => `${spell(n)[0].toUpperCase()}${spell(n).slice(1)}`;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const parts: string[] = [];\\r\\n  if (dueToday > 0) {\\r\\n    parts.push(\\r\\n      dueToday === 1\\r\\n        ? \\"One assignment is due today.\\"\\r\\n        : `${Count(dueToday)} assignments are due today.`,\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  const rest = Math.max(0, openWork - dueToday);\\r\\n  if (rest > 0) {\\r\\n    parts.push(\\r\\n      dueToday > 0\\r\\n        ? `${Count(rest)} more ${rest === 1 ? \\"is\\" : \\"are\\"} waiting.`\\r\\n        : `${Count(rest)} ${rest === 1 ? \\"assignment is\\" : \\"assignments are\\"} waiting for you.`,\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  if (!parts.length) {\\r\\n    parts.push(\\r\\n      unread > 0\\r\\n        ? \\"Nothing is due today.\\"\\r\\n        : \\"Nothing is due today. Drill whatever you feel like.\\",\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  if (unread > 0) {\\r\\n    parts.push(unread === 1 ? \\"One unread message.\\" : `${Count(unread)} unread messages.`);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return parts.join(\\" \\");\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/* �� the week band ������������������������������������������������������ */\\r\\n\\r\\nconst ABBR = [\\"SUN\\", \\"MON\\", \\"TUE\\", \\"WED\\", \\"THU\\", \\"FRI\\", \\"SAT\\"];\\r\\n\\r\\n/** The Monday-based week containing `now`, as seven local ISO dates. */\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Seven days starting TODAY, not Monday to Sunday.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * A fixed calendar week hides tomorrow whenever today is Sunday, which is\\r\\n * exactly when a student most needs to see what is coming: work assigned for\\r\\n * Monday night simply was not on the strip. A rolling window always shows\\r\\n * today first and the next six days, and it re-labels itself as the days pass,\\r\\n * so tomorrow\'s assignment becomes today\'s without anything being rewritten.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function weekDates(now: Date = new Date()): string[] {\\r\\n  const start = new Date(now);\\r\\n  start.setHours(0, 0, 0, 0);\\r\\n  return Array.from({ length: 7 }, (_, index) => {\\r\\n    const day = new Date(start);\\r\\n    day.setDate(start.getDate() + index);\\r\\n    return localDayISO(day);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function weekLabelFor(dates: string[]): string {\\r\\n  const first = parseLocalDay(dates[0]);\\r\\n  const last = parseLocalDay(dates[dates.length - 1]);\\r\\n  if (!first || !last) return \\"\\";\\r\\n  const month = new Intl.DateTimeFormat(\\"en-CA\\", { month: \\"long\\" });\\r\\n  const same = first.getMonth() === last.getMonth();\\r\\n  return same\\r\\n    ? `${month.format(first)} ${first.getDate()} to ${last.getDate()}`\\r\\n    : `${month.format(first)} ${first.getDate()} to ${month.format(last)} ${last.getDate()}`;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Folds every dated thing in the week onto its day. `entries` are already\\r\\n * resolved to a local ISO day by the caller, so nothing is re-parsed here.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function buildWeek(\\r\\n  dates: string[],\\r\\n  entries: { iso: string; label: string; route?: string | null }[],\\r\\n  now: Date = new Date(),\\r\\n): HomeWeekDay[] {\\r\\n  const today = localDayISO(now);\\r\\n  const byDay = new Map<string, { label: string; route?: string | null }[]>();\\r\\n  for (const entry of entries) {\\r\\n    const bucket = byDay.get(entry.iso);\\r\\n    if (bucket) bucket.push(entry);\\r\\n    else byDay.set(entry.iso, [entry]);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  return dates.map((iso) => {\\r\\n    const date = parseLocalDay(iso);\\r\\n    const onDay = byDay.get(iso) ?? [];\\r\\n    const labels = onDay.map((entry) => entry.label);\\r\\n    const weekday = date ? date.getDay() : 1;\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      iso,\\r\\n      abbr: ABBR[weekday],\\r\\n      dayNum: date ? date.getDate() : 0,\\r\\n      isToday: iso === today,\\r\\n      isWeekend: weekday === 0 || weekday === 6,\\r\\n      // One line per cell: the first thing, and a count when there is more.\\r\\n      label:\\r\\n        labels.length === 0\\r\\n          ? null\\r\\n          : labels.length === 1\\r\\n            ? labels[0]\\r\\n            : `${labels[0]} +${labels.length - 1}`,\\r\\n      // The first entry\'s own route, and only its own. ## assistant

The main risks are in the data and inbox plumbing: practice results live in several stores, and the existing inbox hooks don’t share a single cached feed. I’m checking launch routes and notification writers next so the recommendations name concrete changes.

If RESEND_API_KEY or FROM_EMAIL is unset the function returns\\r\\n * a truthful failure - we never fake email_ok.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface NotifyResult {\\r\\n  inbox_ok: boolean;\\r\\n  bell_ok: boolean;\\r\\n  email_ok: boolean;\\r\\n  inbox_error?: string;\\r\\n  bell_error?: string;\\r\\n  email_error?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\ninterface NotifyParams {\\r\\n  adminUserId: string;\\r\\n  studentId: string;\\r\\n  title: string;\\r\\n  /** Path the student should land on, e.g. /classroom/item/<id> */\\r\\n  route: string;\\r\\n  /** Short body shown in the inbox message + bell notification (optional). */\\r\\n  description?: string;\\r\\n  /** Type label used in the email body, e.g. \\"homework\\", \\"bootcamp_module\\". */\\r\\n  assignmentType?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nasync function findOrCreateThread(adminUserId: string, studentId: string): Promise<string> {\\r\\n  const { data: myParts, error: myPartsErr } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(\\"conversation_participants\\")\\r\\n    .select(\\"conversation_id\\")\\r\\n    .eq(\\"user_id\\", adminUserId);\\r\\n  if (myPartsErr) throw myPartsErr;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const myConvIds = (myParts ?? []).map((p) => p.conversation_id);\\r\\n  if (myConvIds.length > 0) {\\r\\n    const { data: shared, error: sharedErr } = await supabase\\r\\n      .from(\\"conversation_participants\\")\\r\\n      .select(\\"conversation_id\\")\\r\\n      .eq(\\"user_id\\", studentId)\\r\\n      .in(\\"conversation_id\\", myConvIds)\\r\\n      .limit(1);\\r\\n    if (sharedErr) throw sharedErr;\\r\\n    if (shared && shared.length > 0) return shared[0].conversation_id;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { data: conv, error: cErr } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(\\"conversations\\")\\r\\n    .insert({ subject: null, created_by: adminUserId })\\r\\n    .select()\\r\\n    .single();\\r\\n  if (cErr || !conv) throw cErr ?? new Error(\\"Failed to create conversation\\");\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { error: pErr } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(\\"conversation_participants\\")\\r\\n    .insert([\\r\\n      { conversation_id: conv.id, user_id: adminUserId },\\r\\n      { conversation_id: conv.id, user_id: studentId },\\r\\n    ]);\\r\\n  if (pErr) throw pErr;\\r\\n\\r\\n  return conv.id;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function notifyStudentOfClassroomItem(\\r\\n  params: NotifyParams,\\r\\n): Promise<NotifyResult> {\\r\\n  const { adminUserId, studentId, title, route, description, assignmentType } = params;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const result: NotifyResult = {\\r\\n    inbox_ok: false,\\r\\n    bell_ok: false,\\r\\n    email_ok: false,\\r\\n  };\\r\\n\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    const conversationId = await findOrCreateThread(adminUserId, studentId);\\r\\n    const body =\\r\\n      `New assignment: ${title}\\\\n\\\\n` +\\r\\n      (description ? `${description}\\\\n\\\\n` : \\"\\") +\\r\\n      `View in your Classroom: ${route}`;\\r\\n    const { error: mErr } = await supabase\\r\\n      .from(\\"messages\\")\\r\\n      .insert({\\r\\n        conversation_id: conversationId,\\r\\n        sender_id: adminUserId,\\r\\n        body,\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    if (mErr) throw mErr;\\r\\n    result.inbox_ok = true;\\r\\n  } catch (e) {\\r\\n    console.error(\\"[classroomNotify:inbox] failed\\", { studentId, error: e });\\r\\n    result.inbox_error = e instanceof Error ? e.message : \\"inbox failed\\";\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    const { error: nErr } = await (supabase as unknown as {\\r\\n      from: (t: string) => {\\r\\n        insert: (v: unknown) => Promise<{ error: { message: string } | null }>;\\r\\n      };\\r\\n    })\\r\\n      .from(\\"notifications\\")\\r\\n      .insert({\\r\\n        user_id: studentId,\\r\\n        type: \\"assignment\\",\\r\\n        title: `New assignment: ${title}`,\\r\\n        body: description ?? null,\\r\\n        link: route,\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    if (nErr) throw nErr;\\r\\n    result.bell_ok = true;\\r\\n  } catch (e) {\\r\\n    console.error(\\"[classroomNotify:bell] failed\\", { studentId, error: e });\\r\\n    result.bell_error = e instanceof Error ? e.message : \\"bell failed\\";\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Email step. Failure is recorded but the assignment is not affected.\\r\\n  const emailRes = await sendAssignmentEmail({\\r\\n    studentId,\\r\\n    title,\\r\\n    description,\\r\\n    route,\\r\\n    assignmentType: assignmentType ?? \\"assignment\\",\\r\\n  });\\r\\n  result.email_ok = emailRes.ok;\\r\\n  if (!emailRes.ok) result.email_error = emailRes.error;\\r\\n\\r\\n  return result;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * One-line summary suitable for a toast.\\r\\n *   - \\"Assigned and notified\\"            (all three ok)\\r\\n *   - \\"Assigned, but email failed\\"        (inbox+bell ok, email failed)\\r\\n *   - \\"Assigned, partial notification\\"    (some other partial)\\r\\n *   - \\"Assigned, but notification failed\\" (all three failed)\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function summarizeNotifyResult(r: NotifyResult): string {\\r\\n  if (r.inbox_ok && r.bell_ok && r.email_ok) return \\"Assigned and notified\\";\\r\\n  if (!r.inbox_ok && !r.bell_ok && !r.email_ok) return \\"Assigned, but notification failed\\";\\r\\n  if (r.inbox_ok && r.bell_ok && !r.email_ok) return \\"Assigned, but email failed\\";\\r\\n  return \\"Assigned, partial notification\\";\\r\\n}\\r\\nimport { supabase } from \'@/integrations/supabase/client\';\\r\\nimport type { User } from \'@supabase/supabase-js\';\\r\\nimport { getOrCreateAdminStudentConversation } from \'@/lib/adminConversation\';\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Session-note notifier (build-spec \\u00156). Distinct from\\r\\n * `notifyStudentOfClassroomItem`, which hardcodes \\"New assignment:\\" copy and is\\r\\n * correct only for homework/assignment parts. A session recap is NOT an\\r\\n * assignment, so it gets its own copy and notification type.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Posts a chat message authored AS Joshua into the 1:1 thread and a bell\\r\\n * notification, both pointing at the recap in the student\'s Classroom. Reuses\\r\\n * the existing admin\\u001dstudent conversation resolver and the notifications table.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Non-throwing per channel: returns granular flags so partial success is\\r\\n * surfaced truthfully, never faked. No source labels (\\"AI\\", \\"transcript\\",\\r\\n * \\"Gemini\\") ever appear in the copy.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface SessionNotifyResult {\\r\\n  inbox_ok: boolean;\\r\\n  bell_ok: boolean;\\r\\n  inbox_error?: string;\\r\\n  bell_error?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\ninterface SessionNotifyParams {\\r\\n  admin: User;\\r\\n  studentId: string;\\r\\n  /** Path to the recap, e.g. /classroom/item/<id> */\\r\\n  route: string;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Optional approved chat copy authored in review. When omitted, a short\\r\\n   * default in the coaching voice is used. Never machine-labeled.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  message?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst DEFAULT_MESSAGE =\\r\\n  \'I left a recap from our session in your Classroom. Take a look when you get a chance, then we keep building from there.\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function notifyStudentOfSessionRecap(\\r\\n  params: SessionNotifyParams,\\r\\n): Promise<SessionNotifyResult> {\\r\\n  const { admin, studentId, route } = params;\\r\\n  const result: SessionNotifyResult = { inbox_ok: false, bell_ok: false };\\r\\n  const body = (params.message?.trim() || DEFAULT_MESSAGE) + `\\\\n\\\\n${route}`;\\r\\n\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    const conversationId = await getOrCreateAdminStudentConversation(admin, studentId);\\r\\n    const { error: mErr } = await supabase.from(\'messages\').insert({\\r\\n      conversation_id: conversationId,\\r\\n      sender_id: admin.id,\\r\\n      body,\\r\\n    });\\r\\n    if (mErr) throw mErr;\\r\\n    await supabase\\r\\n      .from(\'conversations\')\\r\\n      .update({ last_message_at: new Date().toISOString() })\\r\\n      .eq(\'id\', conversationId);\\r\\n    result.inbox_ok = true;\\r\\n  } catch (e) {\\r\\n    console.error(\'[sessionBundleNotify:inbox] failed\', { studentId, error: e });\\r\\n    result.inbox_error = e instanceof Error ? e.message : \'inbox failed\';\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    const { error: nErr } = await supabase.from(\'notifications\').insert({\\r\\n      user_id: studentId,\\r\\n      type: \'message\',\\r\\n      title: \'New recap in your Classroom\',\\r\\n      body: \'A recap from your latest session is ready.\',\\r\\n      link: route,\\r\\n    });\\r\\n    if (nErr) throw nErr;\\r\\n    result.bell_ok = true;\\r\\n  } catch (e) {\\r\\n    console.error(\'[sessionBundleNotify:bell] failed\', { studentId, error: e });\\r\\n    result.bell_error = e instanceof Error ? e.message : \'bell failed\';\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  return result;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

(os error 2)\\nsrc/features/rc\\\\lib\\\\rcTypes.ts:153:  | { mode: \'unlimited\' }\\r\\nsrc/features/rc\\\\lib\\\\rcTypes.ts:154:  | { mode: \'per_passage_budget\'; secondsPerPassage: number };\\r\\nsrc/features/rc\\\\lib\\\\rcSession.ts:107:export function isRevealed(s: RcSessionState, mode: RcRevealMode, item: RcSessionItem): boolean {\\r\\nsrc/features/rc\\\\lib\\\\rcHydration.ts:116:  mode: RcRevealMode,\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * A Drill Launch is everything the runner needs to be told in order to start a\\r\\n * drill: which kind of drill, and the name of the particular run. It travels in\\r\\n * the URL and nowhere else.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * It names a run; it does not contain one. The ordered question list, every\\r\\n * attempt, the position and the clock already spent live in the Run Record\\r\\n * (`drillResumeSession`), which the runner adopts whole on resume. That is why\\r\\n * a launch can be short: refresh only ever destroyed the app\'s ability to work\\r\\n * out *which* run the student was in, never the run itself.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * See `docs/adr/0016-a-drill-link-names-a-run-it-does-not-contain-one.md`.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * This module is the codec - `launchDrill` writes a launch to a query string\\r\\n * and `parseDrillLaunch` reads one back, and the pair round-trips. The runner\\r\\n * consumes a launch through `drillRunSpecFromLaunch`; there is no second\\r\\n * grammar and no expansion step.\\r\\n */\\r\\n\\r\\nimport type {\\r\\n  DrillMode,\\r\\n  FullSectionConfig,\\r\\n  TimerMode,\\r\\n  TypeDrillConfig,\\r\\n} from \'@/types/drill\';\\r\\nimport type { DrillResumeIdentity } from \'@/lib/drillResumeSession\';\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Switches that mean the same thing whatever kind of drill is running. Anything\\r\\n * that belongs to one kind lives inside that kind instead, so that a link\\r\\n * cannot ask for a section timer on a Smart Drill.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface DrillLaunchOptions {\\r\\n  /** Where the runner\'s exit sends the student. Must be an app-relative path. */\\r\\n  returnTo?: string;\\r\\n  /** Heading shown while the drill runs. */\\r\\n  label?: string;\\r\\n  /** Open on this question rather than the first. Someone jumping deliberately. */\\r\\n  initialIndex?: number;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Presentation only: render the adaptive Smart-Drill chrome even for a fixed\\r\\n   * queue, so a hand-picked question looks identical to an Auto run.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  present?: \'smart\';\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Which test run and which leg of it this section belongs to. A practice-test\\r\\n * leg is a Section with a run attached, not a kind of its own: it runs, looks\\r\\n * and closes out identically, and the only difference is that something is\\r\\n * waiting on the result. Losing it on refresh is what left a practice test\\r\\n * permanently open, because the close-out had nothing to write against.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface PtLegRef {\\r\\n  runId: string;\\r\\n  legIndex: number;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * What a bare list of questions is carrying on its back.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * The canonical grammar expresses most of this as a kind of its own - a guided\\r\\n * drill is `drill=guided-drill`, not a list of qids with a flag beside it. But\\r\\n * two things still produce a list plus a flag: a link already sitting in a\\r\\n * student\'s inbox, and a button that hands the run over in memory. Both name a\\r\\n * real run, so what they say has to survive being written into the address bar\\r\\n * and read back out.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Every field here is optional except the two a `questions` launch always\\r\\n * knows. Nothing is inferred: a value that was not sent stays absent rather\\r\\n * than defaulting into a different run.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface RunCarry {\\r\\n  mode: DrillMode;\\r\\n  sealed: boolean;\\r\\n  isGuidedDrill: boolean;\\r\\n  guidedDurationMinutes?: number;\\r\\n  /** A classroom homework assignment the list belongs to. */\\r\\n  assignmentId?: string;\\r\\n  guidedSetId?: string;\\r\\n  guidedDrillId?: string;\\r\\n  guidedAssignmentId?: string;\\r\\n  practiceSource?: string;\\r\\n  practiceSlug?: string;\\r\\n  /** Names the Run Record for a bootcamp day rather than for the drawn list. */\\r\\n  resumeIdentity?: DrillResumeIdentity;\\r\\n  ptLeg?: PtLegRef;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type DrillLaunchBody =\\r\\n  /** The app picks. One continuous adaptive run per student. */\\r\\n  | { kind: \'smart-drill\' }\\r\\n  /** A real PT section, optionally timed and optionally sealed. */\\r\\n  | {\\r\\n      kind: \'section\';\\r\\n      pt: number;\\r\\n      section: number;\\r\\n      /**\\r\\n       * The clock this section runs on. Omitted means the standard 35. A\\r\\n       * section can also be sat at 52.5, at 70, or with no clock at all, which\\r\\n       * is how an accommodation is honoured, so this cannot be reduced to a\\r\\n       * number of minutes.\\r\\n       */\\r\\n      timerMode?: Exclude<TimerMode, \'35\'>;\\r\\n      /** Only meaningful with a `custom` timer. */\\r\\n      minutes?: number;\\r\\n      /**\\r\\n       * Answer and Explanation are never rendered, for the whole run. A section\\r\\n       * assigned as a measurement stops measuring the moment the student can\\r\\n       * check question 3 before answering question 4. ## assistant

## 1. FLAWS

The design direction is implementable, but the plan needs corrections to its data contracts, launch behavior, and inbox model. These findings are from repository code; deployed schema and student rendering were not verified. No files were modified.

1. **“Smart Drill filtered to a type” is not an existing launch contract.** Adaptive Smart Drill accepts no type/count parameters; filtered practice is a separate launch kind. Implementing the proposal literally either ignores the filter or changes Smart Drill, which is explicitly out of scope. See [drillLaunch.ts:94](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/drillLaunch.ts:94).  
   **Fix:** Launch type/count selections through `kind: 'filter'`, optionally with the existing smart presentation; keep adaptive Smart Drill as its own action.

2. **The all-practice data gap is confirmed, not merely an open question.** `attempts` covers the LR runner; RC records separately, and bootcamp exercises have separate stores. An active RC student could otherwise receive an empty or misleading performance area. See [sectionFreshness.ts:16](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/sectionFreshness.ts:16), [rcProgress.ts:151](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/rc/api/rcProgress.ts:151), and [supabase-progress.ts:78](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/topics/abstract/lib/supabase-progress.ts:78).  
   **Fix:** Add a source-to-lens matrix before implementation, with source-specific adapters and explicit coverage labels; do not present LR accuracy as all-practice accuracy.

3. **Several proposed inputs are not available in the named shape.** The assignment feed exposes status and display copy, not structured scores or completion timestamps; `trap_pattern` belongs to journal notes, not `wrong_answer_journal`; sections have legacy score/time fallbacks already handled by a reader. See [useAssignedTodoFeed.ts:16](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/hooks/useAssignedTodoFeed.ts:16), [wajNotesService.ts:29](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/wajNotesService.ts:29), and [sectionHistory.ts:25](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/sectionHistory.ts:25).  
   **Fix:** Name the actual readers and joins per lens; never parse subtitle text for scores or treat manual completion as demonstrated mastery.

4. **The rollup cannot produce the specified 30-day metric.** Its buckets are current seven days, previous seven days, and everything older; the last bucket cannot be trimmed to 30 days. It also counts attempt rows, not distinct questions. See [rollup migration:47](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/supabase/migrations/20260710031524_bf477c7f-e3a5-4717-93b8-a4e2c72c69ca.sql:47).  
   **Fix:** Add a bounded Home-specific RPC with explicit repeat/review rules and distinct-question sample counts, preserving the existing Analytics contract.

5. **The selection rules rank activity volume, not usefulness.** Twenty question attempts and one completed section have incomparable units; the same section may drive several redundant cards. “Minimum eight” also leaves undefined what happens with fewer than three eligible lenses, an inactive student, or an unsupported pinned lens.  
   **Fix:** Define normalized per-lens scores, deterministic ties, redundancy suppression, sample rules, and a pinned-insight fallback; show fewer cards when evidence is insufficient.

6. **The inbox already duplicates events across its two sources.** Assignment and recap writers create both a chat message authored as Joshua and a notification. A simple merge displays the same event twice, and sender-based pinning elevates automated assignment notices as personal messages. See [classroomNotify.ts:87](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/classroomNotify.ts:87) and [sessionBundleNotify.ts:48](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/sessionBundleNotify.ts:48).  
   **Fix:** Introduce an event identity and explicit message origin for new writes, with a conservative legacy display policy; preserve the existing message records.

7. **“One hook, two queries, shared mark-read” substantially understates the work.** `useInbox` already exists with many consumers and multiple sequential reads. Messages use participant read timestamps, Home dismissal uses a separate watermark, and notifications use booleans. Independent hook instances do not provide shared optimistic state. See [useUnreadMessageCount.ts:9](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/hooks/useUnreadMessageCount.ts:9), [useInbox.ts:253](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/hooks/useInbox.ts:253), and [useInbox.ts:276](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/hooks/useInbox.ts:276).  
   **Fix:** Add a separately named unified-feed adapter backed by shared cache/provider state; define thread-versus-message rows, badge units, read behavior, and dismissal independently.

8. **The existing notification hook cannot support “every notification” reliably.** It fetches only 30 rows, computes unread count from those rows, but marks *all* unread rows read. Filtering hidden types after that limit can leave visible events inaccessible. New event types also require a database constraint change, not just TypeScript additions. See [useNotifications.ts:55](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/hooks/useNotifications.ts:55), [useNotifications.ts:130](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/hooks/useNotifications.ts:130), and [notification constraint:1](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/supabase/migrations/20260506163026_3253f980-7520-4671-a14e-1a1795ba5985.sql:1).  
   **Fix:** Filter before pagination, fetch a matching unread total, scope bulk-read consistently, and specify each event’s writer, type, destination, and retry/idempotency behavior.

9. **The proposed inventory misses existing student Home functionality.** The current view renders work rows, intercepts timed-section links for briefings, and records report-card viewing. Phase one could remove graded-work visibility before phase three supplies its replacement. See [ClassroomHomeView.tsx:168](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/components/classroom/home/ClassroomHomeView.tsx:168), [ClassroomHomeView.tsx:197](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/components/classroom/home/ClassroomHomeView.tsx:197), and [ClassroomHomeView.tsx:362](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/components/classroom/home/ClassroomHomeView.tsx:362).  
   **Fix:** Add a feature-preservation checklist and retain those paths until their replacements ship; choose “Continue” from eligible work, not an arbitrary hero/feed row.

10. **Pinning has an incomplete authorization and lifecycle contract.** “Admin write, student read own row” omits admin reads for Hub hydration, identity mapping, invalid lens/params handling, and unpinning. This is an implementation gap, not evidence of an existing exploit.  
    **Fix:** Specify admin SELECT/write policies, authenticated student ownership checks, validated lens parameters, server-controlled attribution, unpin behavior, and cross-student RLS tests.

11. **A viewport breakpoint and another shortcut listener are insufficient integration plans.** The finder already registers Ctrl/Cmd+K, lazy-loads banks, and restores/traps focus. A second launcher instance risks opening two dialogs. Two columns must also fit the available Classroom stage when other panels occupy space. See [QuestionFinder.tsx:64](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/components/questionfinder/QuestionFinder.tsx:64) and [QuestionFinder.tsx:148](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/components/questionfinder/QuestionFinder.tsx:148).  
    **Fix:** Use one launcher owner, preserve permission gates and lazy loading, base columns on available width, and require student-view checks at narrow/mid/wide and sidecar-docked sizes, including keyboard focus and reduced motion.

## 2. IDEAS

Ranked by value relative to effort:

1. **Make the launcher an address bar with contextual actions. — Very high value / medium effort**  
   Keep `PrepTest → Section → Question` visible when opened. Once a section is selected, offer **Start section**; once a question is selected, offer **Open question**. Put type/count practice in a separate tab within the same panel. Preserve LR/RC routing through the existing finder and launch codec. This turns dropdown selections into useful actions without another configuration screen.

2. **Add “Flagged” and “Needs review” shelves inside the launcher. — High value / low–medium effort**  
   A student often remembers *why* they need a question, not its address. Show compact address rows with their flag note or review status, searchable alongside PT/section/question. Existing sources: [flaggedService.ts:31](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/flaggedService.ts:31) and [wajNotesService.ts:29](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/wajNotesService.ts:29). Keep this inside the launcher rather than adding a recent-drills card.

3. **Show section freshness and timer choice before launch. — High value / low–medium effort**  
   Annotate LR section options with “24 of 25 questions unanswered” and offer fresh-only filtering. Carry the existing standard, accommodated, custom, or untimed setting into the launch. The machinery exists in [sectionFreshness.ts:46](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/sectionFreshness.ts:46) and [drillLaunch.ts:100](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/drillLaunch.ts:100). For RC, show unknown freshness until its own history is connected.

4. **Give every insight an evidence drawer. — High value / low–medium effort**  
   Beneath “Strengthen questions need attention,” offer **See the questions behind this**. Show source, dates, sample size, and exact question addresses before the practice action. Existing section detail includes question records, breakdowns, and saved analysis: [sectionHistory.ts:25](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/sectionHistory.ts:25). This makes the dashboard a reasoning tool and exposes bad aggregation early.

5. **Offer “Review these” alongside “Try new ones.” — High value / medium effort**  
   A weak-type insight should distinguish understanding the mistakes already made from testing the skill on fresh questions. Use journal review status and saved section question records for the first action; use a filtered launch with explicit exposure filtering for the second. Label repeated work so it cannot masquerade as fresh performance.

6. **Reuse section diagnoses rather than inventing a second diagnostic engine. — High value / medium effort**  
   The app already saves section analysis and has rules for timing, blind review, and confidence errors. Let Home select and summarize an eligible existing finding, then link to its report. See [sectionAnalysis.ts:568](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/sectionAnalysis.ts:568) and [sectionHistory.ts:44](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/sectionHistory.ts:44). That produces more specific guidance and keeps Home consistent with the results page.

VERDICT: REVISE
